### Lane E1 part d: lots on full frames (K-e6, rev 11, Architect 2026-10-11, after the second S-237 stop)

**Why.** The second S-237 shot (f79f91c1) showed four lots while the sim held 263 at tick 624 (Dispatcher's read-only experiment on `plays/E/S-193.py`, seed 1, region 76: core cell 16463 on a smooth slope, worst climb fraction 0.139, econ about 0.70, 60 lots at step 300, 205 at 600; so the growth port is faithful and Q-193-07 is not the cause). The pipe writes `lots_changed` only on delta frames (`csrc/pipe/pipe_tactical.c:267`) yet advances its diff memory `g_lots_prev` on every frame (`:284-285`), so every lot change in the tick before a full frame (`OM_PIPE_FULL_PERIOD` 20) is never reported; the client folds lots only in its delta branch (`autoload/c_bridge.gd:523`) and reads `lots_dump` once at mission begin (`:764`, before any lot exists). A full frame is therefore not full, and real-time play loses one tick in twenty of lot changes. Finding row `FINDING-lots-full-frame`.

**K-e6 (`VG-CONTRACT-E6`, contract piece, pipe frame change, second-Fable audit; depends_on VG-CONTRACT-E5, landed).** `[B R-193-01, R-193-02: the town the player sees is the town the sim grows]` Every full tactical frame carries `"lots"`: the complete lot list in the `lots_dump` row form `[id,x,y,stage,building,owner]`, ascending `id`, `[]` when there are none, written by `write_tactical_economy` in the `full` branch after `strongpoints`; delta frames keep `lots_changed` exactly as today; `g_lots_prev` keeps advancing on every frame (correct once full frames carry everything); `lots_dump` stays as documented (the once-per-mission read is now redundant but harmless; the client keeps it). Size bound: at most `OM_LOT_CAP` 2048 rows of about 30 bytes per full frame, the same order as the units list it already carries. `docs/architecture.md:413` last sentence becomes: "Full frames carry `lots`, the complete list in the same row form, ascending `id` (`[]` when none); the client replaces its table from it." Client: `_reconstruct_tactical` (`c_bridge.gd:504`) in the full branch when the frame has `lots`, sets `_lots` from it through `_lot_row` (replacing the table, not merging; a full frame without the key leaves the table alone, so old stubs stay green) and `_tactical["lots"]` with it; the delta branch is unchanged; `_lots_fetched` and `_fetch_lots` unchanged. Tests first (a different engineer): `test_full_frame_has_no_lots_key` (`test_pipe_vg_e.py:98`) encodes the K-e ruling this retires: renamed `test_full_frame_has_no_lots_changed_key`, docstring 'K-e6: full frames carry `lots`, never `lots_changed`', body unchanged; the `tests/test_c_bridge_vg_e.gd:6-13` header and `c_bridge.gd:666-667` comment gain 'a full frame's `lots` replaces the table'. `tests/transport/test_pipe_vg_e.py` gains (1) the full frame answered by `mission_begin` has a `lots` key equal to the `lots_dump` list (positive control: both empty on a space mission, green today, and `lots_changed` on a delta still present); (2) after `BEGUN_TERRAIN` plus one `tactical_step` `n` 600 (a full frame: 600/`OM_PIPE_FULL_PERIOD` > 0) the frame's `lots` equals the `lots_dump` list that follows, row for row, and is non-empty (red today: no key; the writer confirms on main that this dump is non-empty before writing, section (d), and reports if it is not: an equal-empty pass is vacuous); (3) control, green today and after: the first delta after that full frame carries no row equal to the full frame's row of the same id (every row is new or differs in `stage`, `building` or `owner`). `tests/test_c_bridge_vg_e.gd` gains: a full frame with three `lots` rows replaces a two-row table (red today), a delta still folds (control). `plays/E/S-193.py` unchanged; the clerk re-runs it and re-takes `S-237` into the same review row, which stays "no" until Westley sees the new shot. Owns `csrc/pipe/pipe_tactical.c`, `docs/architecture.md` (that sentence), `autoload/c_bridge.gd`, `tests/transport/test_pipe_vg_e.py`, `tests/test_c_bridge_vg_e.gd`. The Dispatcher pre-flights ownership of `pipe_tactical.c` and `c_bridge.gd` (both appear in landed rows only, to be confirmed by reading `pieces.toml`). Second finding from the same experiment, not fixed here: `density_economy` is read from the first player-owned home-tier region (region 0, tier 2) rather than the mission's region (76, tier 1); register row `Q-193-09` (a: the mission region; b: the player's home region; c: the holder of the objective) for Westley, default (a) nowhere applied until he answers; row `FU-E1-density-economy-region`.

### Lane W: walk routes (`VG-W1`, `VG-W2`; rev 12, Architect 2026-10-11; Westley's go 2026-10-11 on ROADMAP "walk-route fix")

**Why.** `[B R-001-05: the route with the shortest travel time, changing layer wherever that shortens it]` `[B R-004-07: speed / 1.1 up, x 1.1 down]` Each walker calls `om_walk_route` (`csrc/walk_route.c:78`, full-map Dijkstra over up to 280x280 cells, four mallocs, a mask copy and popcount, two `om_terrain_height_at` lookups per edge, no heuristic) at every waypoint, because `om_move_walker_step` (`csrc/tactical_move.c:2143-2147`) keeps only `pts[1]` as the aim and sets `plan_valid = 0` on each arrival (`:2178`, `:2183`), up to `OM_MOVE_WALK_MAX_LEGS` 16 legs per tick. With about 600 walkers that is about 150 ms per tick (measured 2026-10-10; the fixture below re-measures). Routes must not change: the code's own comment (`:2120`) says the replan agrees with the plan, and every stored golden hashes movement.

**W1, the planner (`csrc/walk_route.c` only; owner `VG-W1`).** Same routes, same `seconds`, byte for byte; only cost changes. (1) Scratch arrays (`dist`, `prev`, `heap`, `pos`, the mask copy) live in one static scratch sized at `OM_WALK_CELLS_MAX` 78,400 (`om_internal.h:435-437`) allocated once on first call and reused (no per-call malloc; `om_world` has one planner at a time, the sim is single-threaded per world: `[B determinism contract, CLAUDE.md]`); the popcount and mask copy are taken once per `terrain_epoch` (compare `om_move_terrain_epoch(w)`, a new internal getter in `tactical_move.c` returning `ms->terrain_epoch`, declared in `om_internal.h`, no header change). (2) Per-cell height cache: an `int32_t` array of cell-centre heights filled once per epoch from `om_terrain_height_at`, read by `wr_height` instead of calling the lookup per edge. (3) A* with an admissible Q16 heuristic: Chebyshev cell distance times the minimum flat step cost (`WR_COST_FLAT_Q16`, the smallest of the three step factors at `walk_route.c:115-121`), tie-break on the Q16 `f` then on cell index ascending (so the settled order and therefore `prev` are deterministic on every platform; integer math only). Route equality is the contract: the parity test below must stay green; if any fixture route differs the builder STOPs (not a tune). Named draft `DRAFT-walk-tick-ms-w1` 10 ms. Tests first, a different engineer, `tests/csim/test_VG_W_w1_planner.py`: positive control, recorded on origin/main before the build, 24 fixtures (seeds 1 to 6, four (start, goal) pairs each on `om_terrain_generate` ground, canvas 24,000 wu) of `om_walk_route` outputs (`pts` and `seconds`) stored as a golden table in the test file by the test writer (the recording run is the control, green today); the red case: the fixture from `tests/csim/test_E01_T2_walker_waypoint_arrival.py:60-67, 128-147` with 600 walkers (`om_roster_set_movement_kind` walking, `om_unit_spawn` on a walkable layer in a 25x24 grid, one `om_command_move` to the far corner), median of 20 `om_world_step` after 5 warm-up through `tests/csim/scale/_scale_helpers.py:93 median_ns`, asserted under `DRAFT-walk-tick-ms-w1` (red today at about 150 ms, the measured value pasted in the report); a second control that the 600-walker run's `om_world_checksum_full` at tick 200 equals the pre-build value recorded by the test writer (routes unchanged). No golden moves; the `FLYER_SECTIONS_200` pin (`test_E01_T2_walker_waypoint_arrival.py:46`) stays.

**W2, stored routes (`csrc/tactical_move.c`, `csrc/save.c` version line, `docs/architecture.md` save paragraph; owner `VG-W2`, depends_on VG-W1, contract piece: save format 10 to 11, second-Fable audit).** `om_move_unit` (`tactical_move.c:158-194`) gains `uint16_t route_n`, `uint16_t route_i`, `double route_x[OM_MOVE_WALK_PTS]`, `double route_y[OM_MOVE_WALK_PTS]` (`OM_MOVE_WALK_PTS` 512 today; the walker plans once per order or epoch change and walks the stored points; on arrival at point `i` it aims at `i + 1` without replanning; a plan beyond 512 points keeps today's second-call behaviour at `:2144-2147` and stores the first 512). Replan only when `plan_valid` is 0 at order time, the epoch differs, or the destination changed (the three triggers at `:2137`, unchanged); the arrival sites `:2178` and `:2183` advance `route_i` instead of clearing `plan_valid`. Routes remain identical by construction (W1's parity table is the proof: the stored route is the same `pts`). Save: `mv_save_all` (`:3469`) writes `route_n`, `route_i` and the `route_n` points after the plan fields (`:3502-3506`); `om_move_save_read` (`:3582`) reads them and rejects `route_n > OM_MOVE_WALK_PTS` or `route_i > route_n`; the movement hash (`mv_word`/`mv_dbl` at `:3385-3392`) hashes them in the same order; `OM_SAVE_FORMAT_VERSION` 11, `OM_PIPE_SAVE_FORMAT_VERSION` 11, `scenes/load_game/load_game.gd:5` 11, the history comments at `checksum_full.c:34` and `terrain.c:34`. Pins move under one ledger row `TD-VG-W-1` (old 10, new 11): `tests/csim/test_VG_E_ke3.py:82-85`, `test_VG_E_ke5.py:375, 632-634, 642-662`, `test_E01_T3_contract.py:164`, `test_VG_E_contract.py:144`, `tests/test_load_game_wrong_version.gd`, `test_E16_B5_save_version.py` (later-build 11 becomes 12); movement-section checksum pins (`FLYER_SECTIONS_200`, the seed-3 full pins from TD-VG-E-21) re-record once under `TD-VG-W-2` (rule-changed by the save layout, old and new named; units-only goldens must not move, the Goldens step verifies). Named draft `DRAFT-walk-tick-ms-w2` 3 ms for the same 600-walker fixture. Tests first: `tests/csim/test_VG_W_w2_routes.py`: the 600-walker median under 3 ms (red), a save round trip at tick 50 mid-walk restores `route_n`, `route_i` and the points and the walk continues to the same checksum at tick 200 as an unsaved run (red: fields absent), a terrain change (`om_map_block_rect`) mid-walk replans (control green today). Owners: W1 `csrc/walk_route.c`, `tests/csim/test_VG_W_w1_planner.py`; W2 `csrc/tactical_move.c`, `csrc/om_internal.h` (the version define and the getter), `csrc/pipe/pipe_internal.h`, `scenes/load_game/load_game.gd`, `docs/architecture.md`, `csrc/checksum_full.c:34` and `terrain.c:34` comments, `tests/csim/test_VG_W_w2_routes.py` and the pinned tests above. All owner rows of these files are attached (pre-flight 2026-10-11); `walk_route.c` last owner TERRAIN-2. Skeleton rule: W1 is a sim-only piece with no look; its shot is the perf line in the landing report; W2 the same.

### Lane F part 1: the Steam-less lobby skeleton (`VG-F-SKEL-A`, `VG-F-SKEL-B`; rev 12, Architect 2026-10-11)

**Why.** `[W Q-F-1, 2026-10-11: "allowed, so long as the test does everything it can to emulate the reality of two players joining and not taking shortcuts beyond what is strictly necessary given the limitation"]` The five lane F plays are red because `scenes/lobby/lobby.gd:82-88` disables Host and Join without Steam and `_launch` (`:292-311`) hardcodes `steam_transport.gd`; no test starts a match through the lobby, and the only two-process test (`tests/qa/desktop/net_match_loopback.py`) bypasses it. Westley's rule means: two real game processes, two lobbies, a host and a guest who joins by id, a start that reaches the battle screen in both, and an order that crosses. A one-process solo host is the shortcut to avoid (it can never report ended or stalled, `net_match.gd:123`).

**F-SKEL-A, local transport and lobby backend (new files only; owner `VG-F-SKEL-A`).** `[B R-140-02: input delay 3 ticks]` `[B R-140-03: drop after 30 s]` Two new autoloads under `autoload/net/`: `udp_transport.gd` implements `transport.gd`'s `attach(id)`, `send(from, to, PackedByteArray)`, `poll(id)` over `PacketPeerUDP` on 127.0.0.1 with one port per peer id (`UDP_BASE_PORT` 47100 + id, `DRAFT-udp-base-port`), same packet bytes as the Steam transport hands the session (no framing beyond the datagram); `local_lobby_backend.gd` implements the `setup()` backend seam (`lobby.gd:75`: `create_lobby`, `join`, `set_lobby_data`, `get_lobby_data`, `members`, `send_chat`, `leave`) over the same UDP: the host listens on `LOBBY_PORT` 47000 (`DRAFT-lobby-port`), the lobby id is the string of that port, a guest `join(id)` sends `{"t":"hello","id":<local id>}` and gets the member list and lobby data back, `send_chat` fans out to every member, `members()` is the host's list mirrored to guests, `leave` sends `{"t":"bye"}`; the local id is `OS.get_process_id()` unless `--net-local-id <n>` is given. Tests first (a different engineer), `tests/test_udp_transport.gd` (two transports in one process exchange ten packets in order; a packet to an unattached id is dropped; control: `loopback_transport.gd` does the same, green today) and `tests/test_local_lobby_backend.gd` (host and guest in one process: join returns two members, lobby data round-trips, chat reaches both, leave removes). Owns `autoload/net/udp_transport.gd`, `autoload/net/local_lobby_backend.gd`, the two tests. No existing file changes.

**F-SKEL-B, the wiring and the two-process play (owner `VG-F-SKEL-B`, depends_on VG-F-SKEL-A).** `lobby.gd`: `_attach_steam()` (`:82-88`), when Steam is absent and the command line carries `--net-local` (read with `OS.get_cmdline_user_args()`), calls `setup(LocalLobbyBackend.new(), local_id, version)` instead of disabling the buttons, and `StatusLine` reads "Local test network (no Steam)"; `_launch` (`:305`) builds `UdpTransport` when the backend is local, else `steam_transport.gd` as today; `transport.attach(local_peer)` in `lobby.gd` `_launch`, after `set_peers`, for every transport (the Steam path's attach is a no-op) `[P scenes/lobby/lobby.gd:340]` (Architect ruling); nothing else in the lobby changes. `tests/qa/desktop/plays/F/_play.py`: `start_solo_match` is replaced by `start_pair_match()`: two `GodotGame` instances (`daemon_client.py:99-213`, `Popen` under `xvfb-run -a`, per-instance qa port and `OM_SAVE_DIR`), both launched with `--net-local --net-local-id 1|2`; host goes menu, MULTIPLAYER, Host; guest goes menu, MULTIPLAYER, types the lobby id into `LobbyIdField`, Join; host waits until `qa_state().members` has two entries, clicks Start; both wait for TACTICAL. S-141's play records the host lobby shot with two members (`[B R-141-01]`); S-140's play box-selects on the host, right-clicks, and asserts after 3 s that the guest's `qa_state` units for the host's faction have moved to the same positions as the host's (the order crossed; `[B R-140-02]`); S-142 presses Escape and P on the host and asserts the tick advances on both. S-143 and S-241 stay red (their `owned_eras`, `common_eras`, `team_sizes` keys and the `TeamSize` control are later F pieces, each with its own row; rev 13, lane F part 2) and their docstrings are rewritten to say so; the stale "no lobby" docstrings in all five are rewritten. Shots: the host's S-141 lobby and the host's S-140 battle screen go to `story_reviews/F.md` for Westley; the lane fans out after his verdict. Owns `scenes/lobby/lobby.gd`, `tests/qa/desktop/plays/F/_play.py`, `plays/F/S-140.py`, `S-141.py`, `S-142.py`, `S-143.py`, `S-241.py`, `tests/test_vg_f_w4_lobby.gd` (one new case: `--net-local` enables Host; control: no flag and no Steam keeps it disabled). Not touched: `autoload/qa_daemon.gd` and `daemon_client.py` (VG-GUARD, in flight), `scenes/tactical_c/tactical_view.gd` (VG-E1, in flight; the `net` qa key waits for it: row `FU-F-net-qa-key`). Register: `Q-F-1` row added, closed with Westley's words above.

### Lane F part 1b: the S-140 play rewrite (`VG-F-S140-CLICK`, `TD-VG-F-s140-click`; Architect 2026-10-20 on Westley's go)

**S-140 play rewrite (`VG-F-S140-CLICK`; a test-writer piece, no builder: the play is the test and builders never edit tests; owner files `tests/qa/desktop/plays/F/S-140.py`, `tests/qa/desktop/plays/F/_play.py`, `scripts/specfactory/lane_plays_known_red.txt`; depends_on VG-F-SKEL-B, VG-F-S143, VG-F-S241).** [B R-140-02@56c964: orders are applied at tick+3] [W Q-087-02 (b): "idle units are 'obstacles' to moving units"] [P tests/qa/desktop/plays/F/S-140.py:31-47, :63-65] [P scenes/ground3d/camera_rig_3d.gd:14] [P tests/qa/desktop/plays/F/_play.py:265-316] Today the play drags a box around the own unit nearest the screen centre and shrinks it until at most `UNITS_MAX` (70, `autoload/net/lockstep_session.gd`) units are selected, then right-clicks a fixed screen row (y 900 or 180) under or over the anchor; VG-A18's framing changed which units that box takes (42 of the 185 own units on screen at seed 1; 56 was the old box's selection at 6fdc9d64) and where the click lands, so the unselected own units stand between the group and the click and the group stops in front of them (FINDING-sim-move-blocked-by-idle-own; Q-087-02). The rewrite: (1) selection: `own` is every own unit in `units_px` (185 at seed 1 on main; the receiver rejects an order naming more than `UNITS_MAX` 70 units, `autoload/net/lockstep_session.gd`). The play picks the click side first: of down, up, right, left, the side whose edge of the own-unit pixel extent is farthest from the screen's edge-scroll margin (the most room to walk), ties in that order. A tie is a room difference of at most `DRAFT-s140-tie-px` 1 px (FU-s140-tie: the built play compares floats without an epsilon and landed as built 2026-10-20; the routeline re-shot piece adds the epsilon and exercises the fallback side once). The selected set is the `UNITS_MAX` own units nearest that side (largest y for down, smallest y for up, largest x for right, smallest x for left); the drag is their bounding box padded `DRAFT-s140-box-pad` 12 px and clamped at least `EDGE_SCROLL_ZONE` + 4 px inside the 1920x1080 screen (the zone is 60 px, `camera_rig_3d.gd:14`); if the box takes more than `UNITS_MAX` units (neighbours inside it), the pad shrinks to 0 and then the set shrinks from the far side until the count is at most `UNITS_MAX` and at least 8; the play asserts every selected unit is in `own` and `8 <= len(selected) <= UNITS_MAX` (a new assertion; the old play asserted nothing about its selection). (2) The click: the point `DRAFT-s140-click-offset` 360 px from the selected group's leading edge (the extreme pixel of the selected set on the chosen side) toward that side, clamped inside the same margin; the corridor is the band from the leading edge to the click, as wide as the selected box's side across the walk direction; a corridor is clear when no unselected own unit's pixel lies in it; when it is not clear the play tries the next side in the order above and fails `spec-gap: no clear corridor` naming the blocking unit ids only when all four sides fail. With the front group selected the corridor holds only units ahead of the army, so idle own units behind it never block it (premise measured 2026-10-20: every centroid-based corridor at seed 1 held 14 to 32 idle own units, on main and on 6fdc9d64 alike; Q-087-02's sim piece, not this play, tests walking through idle friends). (3) Arrival and progress: unchanged, `_play.wait_settled` and the progress check at `_play.py:316` (the ordered units' centroid within half its starting distance of the click at a common tick at least `SETTLE_TICKS` 1200 after the order, `DRAFT-s140-settle-ticks`); no threshold moves. (4) The rewrite does not test the sim stall of FINDING-sim-move-blocked-by-idle-own and its docstring says so; that stall's test is the csim test of the Q-087-02 fix piece. Red reason: the play is its own test, red on main today at `_play.py:315` with `the order made no progress: centroid 250.0 from the click at the order tick, 232.1 at tick 1234 (needs at most 125.0)`, and the new selection assertion is red on the old play. Positive control: on the last green landing 6fdc9d64 the same play walks the whole group (the finding row's bisect). Landing: green through the gate's lane plays on main; the landing commit deletes the S-140 line from `lane_plays_known_red.txt` and closes FINDING-S140-move-no-progress with "exposure fixed by TD-VG-F-s140-click". Shot: the existing `after_move_order` record of the S-140 story; it is re-queued for Westley only if the frame changed.

### Lane E1 part e: placement clears (`VG-E1R`) and the starting force (`VG-CONTRACT-E7`); rev 12, Architect 2026-10-11 after Westley rejected drift item 5

**E1r, placement clears instead of refusing (`csrc/economy_place.c`, `csrc/economy_lots.c` (one new internal function), `docs/architecture.md` :401 and :406; owner `VG-E1R`, depends_on VG-E1 (its files return to attached when E1q's lane finishes; the Dispatcher pre-flights)).** `[B R-193-03: "the self-built pieces under it are cleared and the pieces around it are refilled"]` `[B R-168-01: same]` `[W Q-168-01 (c): "free placement, with limits only from terrain"]` `om_economy_place` (`economy_place.c`, 60 lines) keeps `E1E_UNWALKABLE` (terrain, `om_terrain_walkable_at` over the 5x5) and `E1E_FULL`, keeps the rubble refound path, and changes the lot loop at the `E1E_OVERLAP` site: a natural lot (`owner < 0`) whose cell lies inside the 5x5 footprint is removed; a natural lot outside the footprint never refuses; a placed lot (`owner >= 0`) whose footprint overlaps the new footprint still refuses with `E1E_OVERLAP` (`[DRAFT-place-on-placed]`, register row `Q-168-06`: (a) refuse, the default; (b) the new building replaces the old; (c) allowed to overlap). Removal is a new internal `void om_lots_remove_row(om_world *w, uint32_t row)` in `economy_lots.c`, declared in `economy_internal.h`: shifts `lots[]` and `lot_hit_tick[]` down by one, decrements `n_lots`, rebuilds `lot_at[]` from the table (the same loop `build_inputs` uses), leaves `D`, `P` and `active` untouched (D only rises, so the 2-cell ring refills by itself at the next `om_lots_step`: `lot_try_create` needs `lot_at == -1`, not blocked, `D >= 0.10`; the footprint stays blocked by `blocked[]` and does not refill, which is the rule). Pipe: deltas carry no removals; the next full frame (K-e6, at most 20 ticks) drops the row; `docs/architecture.md:401` "a lot is never removed" becomes "a lot is removed only when a placed building claims its cell (E1r); deltas do not report removals, the next full frame does"; `:406` "refusals (unwalkable or occupied cells)" becomes "refusals: unwalkable terrain, a placed building's footprint, the lot cap". Tests first, a different engineer, `tests/csim/test_VG_E_e1r.py`: control (green today) `test_VG_E_e1e.py::test_control_walkable_footprint_is_accepted` repeated on the E1q fixture (ground mission, 300 steps so natural lots exist); red: placing on a cell with a natural lot inside the footprint is accepted (today `E1E_OVERLAP`); the natural lots inside the 5x5 are gone from `om_lot_get_at` and `lot_at` is -1 there; a natural lot in the 2-cell ring survives; after 20 more steps a ring cell with `D >= 0.10` holds a new stage-1 lot while no footprint cell does; `lot_hit_tick` and ids of surviving lots unchanged (shift correctness); two placed buildings 4 cells apart still refuse (Q-168-06 default); determinism: two worlds, same commands, same tables. Existing tests: `test_VG_E_e1e.py::test_same_spot_twice_is_refused` and `::test_overlapping_footprint_is_refused` stand because both place buildings: a lot is placed when its owner is >= 0 or its building is != 0, natural when its owner is < 0 and its building is 0 (natural lots always carry building 0, lot_try_create), so the e1e fixture's owner -1 lots are placed and never cleared (Architect 2026-10-21, implementation; Westley may veto); `test_VG_E_e1q_lots.py::test_a_placed_lots_id_is_unique` stands. The S-237 play gains no step; the next E1 shot after K-e6 shows the port, and E1r's shot is a placement on a grown town (play `plays/E/S-168.py`, placing a building: place over natural lots, shot after the clear, `[B R-193-03]`; E1r owns that play file, Architect 2026-10-13), queued for Westley.

**E7, the starting force (`VG-CONTRACT-E7`, contract piece: header signature and ABI 7 to 8, second-Fable audit; owner files `csrc/ouroboros_sim.h`, `docs/northstar/spec_factory/signatures.toml`, `csrc/tactical_support.c`, `csrc/mission.c` (spawn order and the mission slot save: `om_mis_net_save_size`, `om_mis_net_save_write`, `om_mis_net_save_read`, registered at `mission.c:3378-3380`), `csrc/om_internal.h` (the `OM_SAVE_FORMAT_VERSION` line and the two accessor prototypes below, taken after W2 lands; the version pins listed at W2 move once more under `TD-VG-E-27`, old 11 new 12), `scenes/load_game/load_game.gd:5`, `docs/architecture.md`; depends_on VG-CONTRACT-E5, VG-W2).** `[W 2026-10-11: "players start with at least half-force when a match begins, if not as far down as 20% (specific number depends on the range of possible values for what the simulation currently is at for the specific mission being played, scaled)"]` `[B R-126-04: a player who holds every structure the whole mission fields at least F]` `[B R-218-03: production is paid by the holder's economy, not free]` `[W Q-218-04 (b): the split is dropped and the situation decides]` Signature change: `double om_mission_compute_starting_force(double F, double share)` and `double om_mission_compute_reinforcement_capacity(double F, double share)` where `share` is the player's share of the contested strongpoints at tick 0, `p / (p + e)` from `om_structure_assign_owners` (`mission.c`, `om_mission_place_structures`; `n` neutral excluded; when `p + e == 0` the whole F spawns and the capacity is 0, nothing could produce it; Architect 2026-10-19, under 5 percent). `start = F * frac(share)`, `share` clamped to [0, 1], where `frac(share) = 0.2 + 0.1 * share` for `share <= START_KNEE` and `0.25 + 1.1 * (share - START_KNEE)` above it, `START_KNEE` 0.5 (`[DRAFT-e7-curve-2]`, replaces `DRAFT-e7-floor-knee`; `[W 2026-10-21 C9: "C"]`): the player starts with a quarter of F at a half share of the contested strongpoints, a fifth at zero share (his 2026-10-11 floor) and four fifths at a full share (register row `Q-218-07`; the knee is now only the join of the two lines; the linear 20 to 80 draft `DRAFT-start-fraction` is retired); `capacity = F - start`. Wiring (today both functions are contract-only, nothing calls them): `om_mission_instantiate_map` spawns the player's force after `om_mission_place_structures` (so `p` and `e` are known), spawning `start_units` units of the composed roster: per unit type `n_t = floor(count_t * start_units / F_units + 0.5)`; the remainder (positive or negative) lands on the type with the largest `count_t`, lowest type index on ties, so the sum is `start_units` and the mix stays the roster's; `start_units = floor(start + 0.5)`, `reinforce_cap = F_units - start_units`, integer arithmetic in `mission.c` only, the capacity signature stays double in, double out; and recording `reinforce_cap` in `om_mission_state` (`om_mission_state` holds `uint32_t reinforce_cap, reinforce_spawned`; accessors `uint32_t om_mission_reinforce_cap(const om_world *w)` and `void om_mission_reinforce_note_spawn(om_world *w)` declared in `csrc/om_internal.h`, with `signatures.toml` rows and `EXPECTED_MODULE` lines (sig-rows rule); `reinforce_cap` is `UINT32_MAX` on a bare world with no mission set up or on a mission whose composed roster is empty (`F_units` 0), documented at the accessor in `om_internal.h`; the accessor returns the allowance left (cap minus spawned); saved in the mission slot: `[B determinism]`, save format bump folded into W2's 11 if W2 lands first, else 11 here; whichever lands second takes 12; the Dispatcher orders them); `om_sup_structure_tick_production_driver` (`tactical_support.c` ~:846) spawns a unit for the player's faction only while `reinforce_spawned < reinforce_cap`, incrementing it; the enemy's production is unchanged (its force is sized by `enemy_total`, R-074). Economy payment (R-218-03) stays E2 (row `FU-E7-production-paid`), and is said so in `architecture.md`. Bible: `R-218-06`'s "until S-211 the starting force is the whole force and the reinforcement capacity is 0 (…liberty, Architect 2026-10-10)" is rewritten to "until S-211 the player starts with a quarter of F when they hold half of the contested strongpoints at the start, sliding down to a fifth when they hold none and up to four fifths when they hold them all, and the structures can produce the rest (Westley, 2026-10-11 and 2026-10-21; curve DRAFT-e7-curve-2)" by the docs piece of E7 (rewritten again 2026-10-21 under C9) (rewrite, never append). Tests first: `tests/csim/test_VG_E_ke2.py:110, 117` pins move under `TD-VG-E-28` (old F and 0; new: `starting_force(1000, 0.5) == 250`, `capacity(1000, 0.5) == 750`, `starting_force(1000, 0) == 200`, `starting_force(1000, 0.25) == 225`, `starting_force(1000, 1) == 800` (curve DRAFT-e7-curve-2; the pins as landed at 7e463db3 were 600, 400, 200, 500, 800 and move under `TD-VG-E-36`)); `test_VG_E_e1h.py:307` moves under `TD-VG-E-28` (argtypes and the call take `share`; the assertion is unchanged); new `tests/csim/test_VG_E_e7.py`: on a ground mission with the greek placeholder era, the player's unit count at tick 0 equals `round(start)` for the fixture's share (premise dumped), production spawns stop at `reinforce_cap` (step until the structure timer would have spawned one more; count unchanged), the enemy keeps producing (control), a save round trip keeps `reinforce_cap` and `reinforce_spawned`; `reinforce_cap` and `reinforce_spawned` are saved AND hashed in `om_mis_net_hash` (after `player_units`): `reinforce_spawned` gates future spawns, so an unhashed divergence would be a silent desync; `test_VG_E_e7.py` gains one test: two worlds differing only in `reinforce_spawned` give different `om_mis_net_hash` values (positive control); ABI pins move 7 to 8 under the same TD row (the list at K-e5 above). Goldens: units-section goldens move in every era (structures are placed in every era, `mission.c:2765`), `SPACE_GOLDEN` and the other ids the `TD-VG-E-26` row lists (rule-changed by Westley 2026-10-11; the F1/F2/F3 and E01_T2 goldens do not move, their scenes have F = 0, corrected Architect 2026-10-20). E7 re-records no golden; VG-E-startforce re-records once under `TD-VG-E-26`; E7 lists the failing ids known-red meanwhile (`scripts/specfactory/gd_tests_known_red.txt`, each with ` :: TD-VG-E-26`). ### Lane H part 3: the spec provenance lint (`HARNESS-provenance`; method rev 3, Westley approved 2026-10-11)

`[W 2026-10-11: "#14: approved"]` One engineer piece, tests first by another. `scripts/specfactory/spec_provenance.py <spec.md>` exits 1 and prints `file:line: <reason>` for: (1) a behaviour sentence without a provenance tag, where a behaviour sentence is one inside a `###`/`**piece**` section that contains a number with a unit or a bare number outside code spans, or one of the words "when", "then", "shows", "sees", "draws", "refuses", "accepts", "default", and a tag is `[W Q-… ]`, `[W <date>: "…"]`, `[B R-…]`, `[P <path>…]` or `[DRAFT-<name>]` within the same paragraph (a tag covers its paragraph); (2) the words "liberty", "as the builder drafted", "Architect default" anywhere; (3) a `[P …]` paragraph with no `file:line` citation; (4) a `[B R-id]` whose id is absent from `docs/bible/epics/*.md`; (5) a `[W Q-id]` whose register row is not closed; (6) a `[DRAFT-name]` with no ledger or register row of that name (`docs/northstar/plans/push_ledger_2026-09-22.md`, `docs/northstar/ambiguity_register.md`); (7) two sentences in one spec naming the same piece id with "refuse" in one and "clear" in the other, or "ring" in one and "never form rings" in a cited rule (the self-contradiction check: implemented as a per-piece-id keyword clash table, `CLASH_PAIRS`, extended as found). `scripts/specfactory/pre_gate.sh` and the Preflight phase of every workflow script run it on the spec the script names; a failure is a STOP before launch. Tests `tests/specfactory/test_spec_provenance.py`: positive control, a ten-line fixture spec with tags passes; red cases one per reason; the acceptance case: run on `docs/northstar/plans/2026-10-07-visible-game-spec.md` at 490803a2 (rev 10, committed fixture copy under `tests/specfactory/fixtures/spec_rev10.md`) it reports at least 39 untagged behaviour lines including lines 146, 149 and 86 (the counts from the 2026-10-11 provenance audit), and reason (7) fires on the E1e pair (117 versus 149). Owns `scripts/specfactory/spec_provenance.py`, `tests/specfactory/test_spec_provenance.py`, `tests/specfactory/fixtures/spec_rev10.md`, the one line in `pre_gate.sh` (its owner HARNESS row is attached). The rev 3 doc edits (the "Workflow specs" bullets in `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/northstar/plans/workflows_2026-10-07/rules.md` sections (c) and (d): audit provenance column, test briefs quote the rule, prototype copies ship a parity test) are a docs piece by the record clerk citing Westley's approval, landed with this spec. Tagging the whole current spec is the Architect's own piece, section by section, after the lint lands (rev 13); new paragraphs from rev 11 on carry tags already.

**HARNESS-red-evidence (lane H; Tool: Workflow; owner `scripts/specfactory/red_check.py`, `scripts/specfactory/merge_gate.sh` (one new stage `red_evidence`, after gate-base lands), `docs/northstar/plans/workflows_2026-10-07/rules.md` section (d), new `tests/specfactory/test_red_evidence.py`; depends_on HARNESS-gate-keep-red-evidence).** [P scripts/specfactory/pre_gate.sh:102-104] [P scripts/specfactory/red_check.py:1-6] rules.md section (d) gains the bullet 'before reporting, run `red_check.py --evidence <piece> --base <sha> <test files>` and commit the file it writes on the tests branch'; no script is wired and `pre_gate.sh` is unchanged; the test writer under that rule commits `docs/northstar/plans/push/red_evidence/<piece>.txt` on the tests branch: the `pytest -rA -q` output of the red run on the built tree (the base of the build), with the failing assertion lines, written by `red_check.py`. File grammar: line 1 `piece: <id>`; line 2 `base: <sha>`; then one `RED <pytest node id>` line per failing test and one `GREEN <node id>` per passing one (controls among them), then a line `---`, then the raw `-rA` output; a piece with no new tests writes the single line `none`. The gate parses only the lines before `---`. `red_check.py --evidence <piece id> --base <sha> <paths>` runs the same pytest with `-rA` added, writes the file above to `docs/northstar/plans/push/red_evidence/<piece id>.txt` and keeps today's exit semantics (non-zero when any new test passes). The gate's `red_evidence` stage REJECTs a tests-first piece whose file is missing, whose listed red ids are not all green on the landing tree, or whose controls (ids containing "control") were not green in the red run; the stage reads `red_evidence/<PIECE_ID>.txt` for each id in the gate's piece list; `none` is accepted only when `git diff --name-only --diff-filter=A <base>...HEAD` adds no file under `tests/`; otherwise `none` is a REJECT 'evidence says none but the diff adds tests: <files>'; the re-run of the listed RED ids is a separate `pytest <ids> -q` on the landing tree, not the acceptance stage. Tests first: fixture evidence files for the three cases; positive control: today's gate has no such stage, so a piece with no evidence file passes it. Closes FINDING-red-first-unverified. Row: HARNESS-red-evidence, queued, wave 15, acceptance tests/specfactory/test_red_evidence.py, comment "# Tool: Workflow".

Tests first: fixtures at 199 and 201 lines and at 79 and 81 asserts; positive control: today's lint warns at 201 and never rejects. `merge_gate.sh` step 12 (mg.sh:663-674) becomes `reject size_lint` when size_lint exits 1 and `warn` otherwise (the comment at :666-670 is rewritten to name DRAFT-size-reject-lines and this piece); owner: this row gains `scripts/specfactory/merge_gate.sh`, ordered after HARNESS-look-rejects (the last queued mg.sh owner in wave 15) through depends_on; test: a fixture branch over the cap gives a `GATE size_lint REJECT` line. ### Rev 12b rulings on the script drafters' gaps (Architect 2026-10-12; each amends the paragraph it names)

**HARNESS-provenance.** [P scripts/specfactory/pre_gate.sh:163-174] The pre-gate line lints the spec of every piece row in the piece list (the row's `spec` key) and also the file `PRE_GATE_SPEC` names when it is set (`[DRAFT-pregate-spec-env]`); every workflow Preflight sets it from rev 13 on, when the current spec is tagged. `tests/specfactory/test_pre_gate.py` gains one case (an untagged fixture under `PRE_GATE_SPEC` makes `pre_gate.sh` exit non-zero), so that file joins the owns list. Output line `<path>:<line>: (<n>) <text>`. The unit of a behaviour sentence is a heading, a list item or a blank-separated paragraph, whole words; a heading that claims approval with no `file:line` fails by reason (3), which is why rev 10 line 86 counts. The repo root is the first ancestor holding `docs/bible/epics`. `CLASH_PAIRS` starts with the two pairs given; a piece id is the bold token opening a paragraph, pattern `^\*\*([A-Z][A-Za-z0-9-]{1,23})[, (]`; "cited rule" is the R-id text read from the epic file. The acceptance case asserts at least 39 reports on the rev 10 fixture; fewer is a STOP that says the lint is weaker than the audit, not a tune. Owner row depends_on HARNESS-pregate and A94-harness.

**HARNESS-provenance-scope (lane H; Tool: Workflow; owner `scripts/specfactory/spec_provenance.py`, the one `spec_provenance.py` call line of `scripts/specfactory/pre_gate.sh`, new `tests/specfactory/test_spec_provenance_scope.py`; depends_on HARNESS-C4).** [P scripts/specfactory/spec_provenance.py:334-344] [P scripts/specfactory/pre_gate.sh:168] `spec_provenance.py` judges the whole spec file for every piece row that has a `spec` key, so findings in paragraphs that belong to other pieces reject a piece's pre_gate. Change: `spec_provenance.py <spec.md> --pieces ID[,ID...]` computes every reason over the whole file as today and prints only the findings whose line lies inside a paragraph that names one of the listed ids; without `--pieces` the output is byte for byte today's, and `--pieces ""` is an empty list, not a usage error. A paragraph names an id when its opening is the bold token `**ID` followed by a space, a comma or an open parenthesis, or a list item whose opening is `- ID` or ``- `ID` `` ([DRAFT-provscope-forms]); a paragraph matched by two ids counts once. With `--pieces` the lint prints, after the findings, one line `PROVSCOPE <path> pieces=<ids> paragraphs=<k> in-scope=<n> whole-file=<N>` (the whole-file count is information); when no listed id names a paragraph the whole file is checked as today, every finding is printed and no WARN line is printed ([DRAFT-provscope-nomatch]); when at least one id matched, each listed id that names no paragraph gets one line `PROVSCOPE WARN <path> id=<id> opens no paragraph`, and a WARN line never changes the exit code. `pre_gate.sh` passes its piece list as `--pieces` to every spec it lints, so ids that belong to another spec print WARN lines and never reject. The cite rule (reason 10): a number is not a finding when a `[P file:line]` tag appears anywhere in the same unit (the lint's unit: a heading, a list item with its continuation lines, or a blank-separated paragraph) as the number, before or after it; a `[P ...]` tag with no `file:line` does not count, a tag in another unit does not count, and reasons 3, 8 and 9 are unchanged. Tests first: a spec with one clean and one dirty paragraph under their own piece ids and a third paragraph of another piece, `--pieces` of the clean id passes and of the dirty id rejects; positive control: today's whole-file mode rejects on the dirty and the third paragraph. Row: HARNESS-provenance-scope, queued, wave 13, acceptance tests/specfactory/test_spec_provenance_scope.py, comment "# Tool: Workflow". A plain `- ID` list item takes the bold form's follower set (space, comma, open parenthesis) plus end of line; the backticked ``- `ID` `` form is closed by its backtick.

**W1.** W1 owns the three-line getter `om_move_terrain_epoch` in `csrc/tactical_move.c` and its one declaration in `csrc/om_internal.h` (internal, no `signatures.toml` row); W2's owns list loses "and the getter". `OM_WALK_CELLS_MAX` is `OM_TERRAIN_MAX_CELLS * OM_TERRAIN_MAX_CELLS` and `WR_COST_FLAT_Q16` is defined in `walk_route.c`. A* is dropped from W1: an A* tie-break cannot promise today's path on equal-cost ground, and route equality is the contract; W1 is scratch reuse, the height cache and the per-epoch mask cache, pure Dijkstra, same settle order; if 10 ms is not met the perf test's measured line goes to the Architect for a W1b ruling (an A* piece with a recorded-route re-ruling), not a widening. The cache key is (world pointer, `terrain_epoch`, layer, `cells_w`, `cells_h`); any other key is a STOP. `OM_WALK_OPEN_CAP` is untouched. The test writer chooses and lists the fixture parameters. The 10 ms draft is wall-clock median. The tick-200 control compares every unit's (x, y, layer) from `om_unit_get_*` to a table the writer records, not `checksum_full` (the economy block moves with other landings). Arrival sites are `:2175` and `:2180` on main. Rows VG-W1, VG-W2 and the lane W table row are the record clerk's.

**F-SKEL.** (1) The local backend has `poll()`; `lobby.gd` `_process` (`:314`) calls it when the backend is local, and the backend's events call the same handlers the Steam signals reach (`:95-99`: members changed to `_refresh`, chat to `on_chat`, lobby created to `_on_lobby_created` setting `LobbyIdField`, `StatusLine` and `be.lobby_id`); that pump is inside F-SKEL-B's `lobby.gd` scope and nothing else in the lobby changes. (2) `UdpTransport.set_peers()` exists as a no-op. (3) The local backend is `RefCounted`; `NetMatch` holds its `send_chat` Callable, which keeps it alive after the lobby scene frees. (4) The two files are plain scripts under `autoload/net/`, loaded with `load(path).new()`, no `class_name`, no `project.godot` change. (5) The record clerk adds VG-F-SKEL-A/B, the two test files, creates `story_reviews/F.md`, and notes VG-B's `shots/**` glob as a pre-existing overlap (not a collision: the landing clerk writes shots). (6) S-143 and S-241 go into `lane_plays_known_red.txt` with the rows of the later F pieces that build their keys (red by design, unlike the A plays, which are a finding). F-SKEL-A and B are one landing. (8) The lobby port and the UDP base port are command-line arguments `--net-local-lobby-port <n>` and `--net-local-udp-base <n>` with the draft defaults; the play passes free ports from `pick_free_port` so concurrent runs never collide. The guest game runs at 1280x720 (the shots are the host's at 1920x1080) to stay under the memory cap; both games boot before either answers the privacy modal; the lobby id is typed as digit keys; `qa['net']` assertions become tick and position checks (positions compared after the ordered units stop); both peers at faction 0 is accepted for the skeleton (row `FU-F-guest-faction`). (9) The display waiver is ledger row `FU-B22-pregate-display` (branch `push/vg-b22-rulings` c4b0db24); if it is not on main the record clerk lands it from there.

**B23.** The pin row is `TD-VG-B-14`. It also moves `tests/qa/desktop/galaxy_camera_probe.gd` `qa_camera` range 60..140 to 30..70 (`SETTLE_SEC` 4.0 is a wait, untouched), `galaxy_labels_probe.gd` `OVERVIEW_DISTANCE` 150 to 75, and the A56 table: every distance halved, splits 35 and 16.25. "Faction 0" is the player's faction (`CBridge` player faction id), value 0.95; owner -1 is the neutral grey (0.55, 0.55, 0.58); other owners keep the golden-angle hue at 0.65/0.85. The flash becomes the prototype's billboard quad 1.5 for 0.6 s. `field_bake.gdshader:25` stays (inert). `VG-B23` owns `docs/bible/epics/E-16-client-camera-hud-and-world-view.md` line 109 for the band rewrite. Main's line numbers are the ones to use where the paragraph cites main. The display waiver extends to `tests/test_galaxy_view.gd` and `tests/test_vg_b_b23.gd` under the same FU row. **Rev 12c (2026-10-12), batch 2.** Provenance: a piece id is any whole token matching `VG-[A-Za-z0-9-]+`, `[A-Z]\d{1,2}[a-z]?(-[a-z0-9]+)?`, `K-[a-z]\d?` or `HARNESS-[a-z-]+`; units sharing a token are clash candidates; "claims approval" means the whole words approved/approval; with no `docs/bible/epics` ancestor the lint reads its own repo. W1: the terrain state gains an unsaved, unhashed `uint32_t gen_id`, incremented in `om_terrain_generate` and `ts_save_read` (W1 owns those lines and the field); the cache key is (world pointer, `gen_id`, `terrain_epoch`, layer, `cells_w`, `cells_h`); `terrain_epoch` is hashed, so W1 adds no bump of its own (the generate path already bumps it through `om_move_set_canvas_size`, `tactical_move.c:562`, measured 3 after prepare+generate, then 5, 7, 9 on regenerates in place; `gen_id` alone covers free-and-recreate at the same address and load over a live world, both tested). The gen_id invisibility test zeroes the per-process world serial (`u64` at save offset 40, `save.c:310`) before hashing: a mask of a non-deterministic field, not a tolerance. B23: the player faction is `snapshot['player_faction']`; the clerk skips the main push when the only gate rejects are the four `FINDING-gd-red-main-2026-10-12` tests; probe pins keep each asserted relation after halving. F-SKEL: the local backend declares Steam's four signals with the same arguments, emitted from `poll()`, plus `local_id()` and a `set_local_id(n)` test seam; lobby ids are ints (the port); chat travels as `var_to_str` text in the JSON frame; the play draws the lobby port and a UDP-confirmed block of four base ports from `pick_free_port`; unit tests read `OM_TEST_PORT_BASE` (default 47000, UDP base +100) and the gate sets a distinct base; rows `FU-F-s143-common-eras`, `FU-F-s241-teamsize` carry the known-red entries; the merge gate's suite stage runs the two display tests under the xvfb line for this landing.

### The four red client tests on main (ruling 2026-10-12, from the read-only scout)

`tests/test_vg_e_e1p.gd` E1p-b group: red by design, written ahead of the PlacementUi piece (VG-E1 `tests_pending`); stays red until E1p-b builds. `tests/test_time_jump_replay.gd:261`: a wall-clock tolerance (1.0 s on a 25 s live replay) under machine load 9 to 11; not a tolerance change: the gate reruns it alone once when it fails and records both times (`FINDING-time-jump-wallclock`); two failures alone is a reject. `tests/test_tactical_camera.gd` (A6a-1, `[B R-103-01]`: at mission start 23 of 236 own units sit outside the 1920x1080 viewport; red since at least 4a8df114, 2026-10-09, never ruled) and `tests/test_tactical_delta_reconstruction.gd:166` (after a unit dies the client's delta-built view differs from the server's full frame by one unit; red since at least 4a8df114, never ruled): real findings, rows `FINDING-camera-start-framing` and `FINDING-delta-death-unit`; the gate lists them by name as pre-existing red with those rows (not a skip, not a known-red entry) until their pieces land: **A18** (owner `VG-A18`, `scenes/tactical_c/tactical_view.gd` `_framing_zoom` :978-995, after A16, before VG-E1's remaining pieces: the opening frame fits every own unit inside the viewport with one tenth of the viewport, [DRAFT-a18-margin], clamp at ZOOM_MAX/ZOOM_STEP, `[B R-103-01]`; the existing test is the red test; depends_on VG-A16) and **K-d1** (owner `VG-K-D1`; `[B R-133-06]`, K-4/K-7 rows; the existing test is the red test; the read-only scout's settled finding: the pipe is the side that drops it. `csrc/pipe/om_pipe.c` `handle_tactical_step` calls `ev_reset()` before the step loop even when `n == 0` (`pipe_tactical.c:50-54` memsets `g_fired`, which drives state bit 1), so a non-stepping full request loses the fired flag. Fix: guard `ev_reset` with `n > 0` (`n < 0` is malformed and treated as 0, said so in a code comment). The same piece rewrites `docs/architecture.md` :376 and :392 to "bit 1: fired during the most recent stepping call (n > 0). A call with n == 0 (a resync frame) repeats the previous stepping call's state bits and events unchanged." `VG-K-D1` owns `om_pipe.c` `handle_tactical_step`, the new transport case in `tests/transport/test_pipe_tactical_delta_frame.py` and `docs/architecture.md`; `autoload/c_bridge.gd` and `csrc/pipe/pipe_tactical.c` stay untouched; follow-up `FU-K-d1-net-step`).

Two thresholds, not one `[A 2026-10-12, Architect ruling]`: the cliff test is `CLIFF_SLOPE` 0.22 `[P settlement_sim.gd:19, is_cliff :172]`; the yaw threshold that turns a lot to the contour is `CONTOUR_SLOPE` 0.06 `[P settlement_sim.gd:32]`; slopes are read under the field-map/sim formula (`field_map.gd:380-402`, `economy_density.c:73-90`). The writer builds a fixture that exceeds 0.22 under that formula (a cliff lot); the existing CLIFF fixture reads 0.048 under it (mean over four neighbours, `[P csrc/economy_density.c:75-90]`; not 0.096 `[A 2026-10-13, Architect ruling]`), so it is the negative control for both thresholds: not a cliff (below 0.22) and, below 0.06 too, no turn to the contour; the writer adds a fixture above 0.22 (the cliff lot) and one between 0.06 and 0.22 (the yaw positive case); a flat fixture under 0.06 is the negative control for the yaw rule. Tests first quote each number above; parity test: a frame of the port beside the mockup at the same density field, disc of lots compared by stage histogram and by the fraction of lots whose drawn box is not axis-aligned (method rev 3). **VG-C-unit-ground-fit (lane C, after VG-C-unitscale; owns `scenes/ground3d/ground_units.gd` and `tests/test_vg_c_unit_ground_fit.gd`; Architect 2026-10-22, implementation, Westley may veto on the next lane C shot). Tool: Workflow (one piece, ends in a frame).** [W 2026-10-22: "The unit's placeholders appear to be clipping into the ground. That's ok for now, but that's something we'll want to fix for beta."] [P scenes/ground3d/ground_units.gd:29-45] [P scenes/ground3d/ground_units.gd:72-83] [P scenes/ground3d/ground_pick.gd:13] Today `set_units` places each body at `Vector3(x, y_for(...), z)` with `gy = GroundPick.height_at(terrain, x, z)` (:72, :82) and rotates it by facing only (:83); the body is a horizontal polygon of radius `BODY_RADIUS_WU` 11.5 (:29-45), so on a slope its uphill half lies under the ground. This piece makes a walker's body follow the ground: `set_units` samples `GroundPick.height_at` at (x + r, z), (x - r, z), (x, z + r), (x, z - r) with r = `BODY_RADIUS_WU`, takes the normal of the two difference vectors (dx = (2r, h(x+r) - h(x-r), 0), dz = (0, h(z+r) - h(z-r), 2r), n = dz x dx normalised; flat ground gives (0, 1, 0)), and sets the body's transform so its local up is n and its local forward is the facing projected onto the plane of n (local +x is forward: forward = (cos f, 0, sin f) projected onto the plane of n; Basis x = forward, y = n, z = x cross y; no look_at, which needs a target), with the body lifted by `DRAFT-unit-ground-lift` 1.0 wu along n. Flyers are unchanged (their y is the layer floor plus the clearance). The `heights()` qa key keeps reporting `position.y`, which on flat ground rises by exactly the lift: `tests/test_vg_c6_ground_units.gd:29` (y) and `:30` (x and z), `tests/test_vg_e_e1k.gd:276-277` and `tests/test_vg_c6b_ground_units_layers.gd:59`, `:69` and `:70` (the control, walker and layer-1 walker `units3d_heights` pins) [P tests/test_vg_c6b_ground_units_layers.gd:59] [P tests/test_vg_c6b_ground_units_layers.gd:69-70], which pin a walker's position to the ground point, move by the lift under ledger row `TD-VG-C-ground-lift` (the writer opens the row; class superseded pin, Architect 2026-10-23); the writer states every pin's new expected value per pin as the lifted point: flat ground y + 1.0; sloped ground the ground point plus n x 1.0 (y rises by cos theta, x and z shift by the normal's horizontal components); tolerances unchanged; `tests/test_vg_c_unitscale.gd` pins no y and is not on the list. The draft is `DRAFT-unit-ground-lift`; the row's size_estimate names it. Tests first (`tests/test_vg_c_unit_ground_fit.gd`): on a fixture terrain with a 30-degree plane (`cells_w` and `cells_h` 8, heights round(5774 cm per column index), units at least 65 wu from any edge: the ground sampler clamps to the cell centre 50 wu in [P scenes/ground3d/ground_pick.gd:19-20], plus body reach 15), for every walker every vertex of its body mesh, transformed to world space, has y greater than or equal to `GroundPick.height_at` at its own x and z (no vertex under the ground); the body's up vector dotted with the plane normal is above `DRAFT-ground-fit-up-dot` 0.99; on flat ground the body's up is (0, 1, 0) and its y is ground plus 1.0 within `DRAFT-flat-y-eps` 0.05; facing still turns the body about n (on the 30-degree fixture, facings f and f + pi give local x vectors whose dot is -1 within 1e-4, each perpendicular to n within 1e-4); positive control: the un-fixed placement (lift 0, up (0, 1, 0)) fails the vertex test on the 30-degree fixture. Out of scope: convex crests and hollows (non-planar ground) may still clip; the vertex test applies to the planar fixture only. Shot: the S-132 close frame re-shot at camera height 300 on the same ridge, queued for Westley with the words "bodies sit on the slope".

The marks sit at `_display_px` from `Ground3DHost.to_screen` (:126-127) with no occlusion test, so units behind a ridge draw through it (FINDING-s001-marks-through-terrain). Two changes. (2) Occlusion: NO rig hooks and no edit to `camera_rig_3d.gd`: `Ground3DHost` (`ground3d.gd`) recomputes `occluded_ids` in `set_frame` (:104) as before and in its own `_process` when the rig camera's `global_transform` differs from the cached one, at most once per `DRAFT-occlusion-recompute-ms` 100 ms while it is changing and once more when it settles; time-sliced, see the Cost guard below. It computes per unit in the frame the ray from the camera's global position (`camera_rig_3d.gd:176 get_camera()`) to `GroundPick.world_to_3d(t, x, y)` and calls `GroundPick.pick(t, origin, dir)` (:40); the pick API is unchanged and returns the hit as `Vector2(x, z)` [P scenes/ground3d/ground_pick.gd:58], so the host turns that into the 3D hit point with `height_at` [P scenes/ground3d/ground_pick.gd:13] and compares the camera-to-hit distance with the camera-to-unit distance itself; when the hit is nearer the camera than the unit's point by more than `DRAFT-occlusion-slack` 20 wu (the slack applies to that difference) the id goes in `_occluded: Dictionary`; `occluded_ids() -> Dictionary` exposes it and `qa_state()` (:145) gains `occluded_ids` (sorted ints), and `tactical_view` `qa_state()` gains `marks_visible`: per unit id `{"body": bool, "ring": bool}`; the test reads it. Interface, fixed in full: `occluded_ids` sits in qa_state's `ground3d` block (sorted ints); `marks_visible` is keyed by decimal-string unit ids, each `{"body": bool, "ring": bool}`; the test drives the rig through its public verbs `zoom_by`, `pan_to` and `set_top_down` [P scenes/ground3d/camera_rig_3d.gd:123] [P scenes/ground3d/camera_rig_3d.gd:142] [P scenes/ground3d/camera_rig_3d.gd:148], waits one frame past the settle and reads `occluded_ids()`. It gains the `arrows` key from the two accessors facing-arrow adds (`qa_state()` key `arrows`, both counts); facing-arrow lands first. The visibility line `node.visible = _ground3d == null or not _ground3d.occluded_ids().has(id)` is guarded by `_space_hosted` (it runs only when the view is not space-hosted; on the space host the line is not reached, `occluded_ids()` is not called and the space path is unchanged) and runs at every site that sets a UnitVisuals node position: `_apply_motion` :961 and the land placement in `_redraw` that VG-C-land-one-projection adds. Terrain re-show (FINDING-lop-terrain-reshow, client, 2026-10-23): `_sync_from_rig` sets the 2D terrain layer visible on the map band [P scenes/tactical_c/tactical_view.gd:405-406], so on land `_redraw` re-hides it on every call; `_sync_from_rig` checks the land flag (one line, owner this piece). Cost guard (Architect 2026-10-23): the 2 ms is a per-frame budget kept by time-slicing: each `_process` picks as many pending units as fit in `DRAFT-occlusion-slice-ms` 2 ms (`Time.get_ticks_usec`), a full pass over all units completes within `DRAFT-occlusion-pass-ms` 500 ms on the S-001 frame (176 units), and `occluded_ids` is swapped in whole when a pass completes; a pass is requested by `set_frame` when any unit's cell changed since the last pass and by the camera-settle check, and a request during a pass restarts it after; per ray a cell DDA (the ray height at each cell boundary against the cell's maximum corner height, a `PackedFloat32Array` precomputed once per terrain, stopping at the first cell whose maximum exceeds the ray; the 20 wu slack applies to the final hit distance taken from one bisection step inside that cell), not `GroundPick.pick`. Tests: on the S-001 frame under Vulkan the maximum per-frame pass cost is under 2 ms over 60 frames, a full pass completes under 500 ms, and `occluded_ids` equals the unsliced oracle. Unchanged tests, stated: `tests/test_vg_a_unit_visuals.gd:198-201` (the ring node exists and keeps its colour; only `visible` changes) and `tests/test_vg_d_view.gd:1253-1258` (space path). Era fixture: the test adds a stub node named `EraConfig` under root whose `kind()` returns "ground" (and "space" for the control); `autoload/era_config.gd:37` is the real one. Tests first (`tests/test_vg_c_land_marks.gd`): on land every unit's LayerRing is invisible and its Body visible; with the era node absent the ring is visible (the space path unchanged); occlusion fixture: 8 by 8 cells, a ridge of 400 wu height along rows 3 and 4 (constant y, a plateau from y = 350 to y = 450, 100 wu wide = two march steps, across the camera's view), camera at the rig default; the fixture keeps its crest at least two pick steps wide so the test does not depend on the pick fix (FINDING-groundpick-march-skips-crest); one unit behind the ridge (occluded: `occluded_ids` holds that id and its 2D node is invisible), one in front (not), one 8 wu behind the plateau's camera-side edge at y = 450 (the measured pick gap there is about 0 wu, inside the 20 wu slack: not occluded, the below-slack control); the test compares the pick distance to the unit distance directly (the control that the pick hit is nearer); positive control: a flat fixture gives an empty `occluded_ids`. Shot: the S-001 ridge at camera height 300, queued for Westley with the words "no marks through the ridge".

**VG-C-groundpick-step (lane C, wave 2, after VG-C-land-marks; owns `scenes/ground3d/ground_pick.gd` and `tests/test_vg_c4_ground_pick.gd`; Architect 2026-10-23, implementation; Tool: Workflow (one piece)).** [P scenes/ground3d/ground_pick.gd:8] [P scenes/ground3d/ground_pick.gd:48] [P scenes/ground3d/ground_pick.gd:59] Finding (FINDING-groundpick-march-skips-crest): `GroundPick.pick` marches the ray at `STEP` 50 wu (`ground_pick.gd:8`, advanced at :59) and tests the terrain only at each step, so a crest thinner than one step can lie between two samples and the ray passes through it; the VG-C-land-marks occlusion fixture keeps its crest two steps wide to avoid it. The one change: `STEP` halves to 25 wu (`DRAFT-pick-step-wu` 25), and the bisection backstep `maxf(s - STEP, 0.0)` (:48) follows the constant. The one test (`tests/test_vg_c4_ground_pick.gd`): a crest thinner than 50 wu, on which a unit behind it today goes unoccluded, now occludes it (the ray hit is nearer the camera than the unit); positive control: the same assertion run with the step at 50 wu fails, so the test is red for the stated reason. This piece carries no numbers beyond these.

**VG-C-facing-arrow (lane C, after VG-C-unit-ground-fit and before VG-C-land-marks; owns `scenes/ground3d/ground_units.gd` (after ground-fit), `scenes/tactical_c/unit_visuals.gd` (before land-marks), `scenes/tactical_c/unit_squads.gd` (after VG-C-land-one-projection, which owns it first; it computes the offset itself at :48 and takes `UnitVisuals.facing_offset(type)`, the one-source offset read), `tests/test_vg_c_unit_ground_fit.gd` (after VG-C-unit-ground-fit, which owns it first), `tests/test_vg_c_facing_arrow.gd`; Architect 2026-10-22, implementation; the size is Westley's). Tool: Workflow (one piece, ends in a frame).** [W 2026-10-22: "tiny little arrows pointing away from the unit that indicate direction (like a white triangle, no bigger than 2/5 the unit size)"] [P scenes/ground3d/ground_units.gd:29-45] [P scenes/ground3d/ground_units.gd:83] [P scenes/tactical_c/unit_visuals.gd:8] [P scenes/tactical_c/unit_visuals.gd:18-25] [P scenes/tactical_c/unit_visuals.gd:99] [P scenes/tactical_c/unit_visuals.gd:180-182] White, own and enemy alike. Geometry: the arrow lies on the ray from the body centre along the unit's heading h = facing + `facing_offset_rad` (the offset by type: raider at facing 0 points at -x, bombard at -y, the four others +x); its base is at distance r along that ray (r is the body radius: `BODY_RADIUS_WU` 11.5 wu in 3D, `SHAPE_RADIUS` 12 px times the body's `k` in 2D) and its apex at base + `DRAFT-facing-arrow-len` 0.4 x the body size along the same ray (length 9.2 wu in 3D, 9.6 px in 2D at k = 1, base width half its length); there is no per-shape front edge. 2D: `_make_node` (:99) gains a `Polygon2D` named `Arrow`, a sibling of Body under the unit's Node2D with `rotation` h (`fac + _offset`, :180-182); Body keeps its own rotation and the arrow is not a child of Body, so the offset applies once. 3D: `_body` (:29-45) gains a child `MeshInstance3D` named `Arrow`, an unshaded white triangle in the body plane lifted 0.2 wu so it does not z-fight, local +x forward and no rotation of its own; the body's Basis already carries h after the ground-fit amendment (`forward = (cos(f+o), 0, sin(f+o))` projected onto the plane of n, `rotation.y = -(facing + offset)` :83 before it). No `qa_state` in this piece; the tests read accessors: `Units3D.arrow_count()` and `Units3D.arrow_apex_world(id)` in `ground_units.gd`, `UnitVisuals.arrow_count()` and `arrow_apex_px(id)` in `unit_visuals.gd`. Tests first (`tests/test_vg_c_facing_arrow.gd`): each unit node in both views has an `Arrow` child, white, its length within 0.1 wu or 0.5 px of the draft; the apex direction equals the unit's facing (a unit facing 0 has its 2D apex at +x of the node, facing pi at -x; in 3D the apex's world offset from the body centre points along the facing); positive control: a unit whose facing changes by a half turn between two frames has its apex on the opposite side. The pitch-90 offset test uses a stationary unit with a planted facing (no dependency on VG-E-facing-travel). Superseded pins under `TD-VG-C-facing-arrow`: `tests/test_vg_c6_ground_units.gd`, `tests/test_vg_c6b_ground_units_layers.gd`, `tests/test_vg_e_e1k.gd` (rotation.y or child inventory; the writer cites lines); `tests/test_vg_c_unitscale.gd` is checked and listed only if red. Shot: the S-001 ridge close frame, "arrows show facing". Amendment 2026-10-22 (FINDING-facing-offset-2d-only): the manifest's `facing_offset_rad` is applied in one place, `UnitVisuals.facing_offset(type)` (new static, reading the resolved manifest the way :59 does), used by the 2D icon rotation (:182), UnitSquads and the 3D body (`ground_units.gd:83`: `rotation.y = -(facing + offset)`); a test asserts the raider's 3D nose and its 2D icon point within 2 degrees of each other on screen at pitch 90; positive control: on main they differ by 180 degrees. Amendment 2026-10-23 (TD-VG-C-facing-arrow-offset): the S-132 forward assertions in `tests/test_vg_c_unit_ground_fit.gd` expected the plain facing; after facing-arrow the body heading carries `facing_offset_rad`, so each pin moves to the exact offset heading projected on the plane, no tolerance change; owner this piece, whose owns gains that test file and `unit_squads.gd` for the one-source offset read; one squad rotation assertion is added to `tests/test_vg_c_facing_arrow.gd`.

**PROTO-ground-relief (prototype, lane C look; owns `scenes/ground3d/ground3d.gd` (lighting lines :44-53), `scenes/ground3d/ground_mesh.gd` (vertex colour :29, material :34-38), `scenes/ground3d/ground_bands.gd` (:5-6, :9-11), on a branch only: nothing lands before Westley's pick; Architect 2026-10-22). Three variants on one branch, selected by a build-time constant `RELIEF_VARIANT` in `ground_mesh.gd` (a, b, c; default a; removed at integration): (a) slope shading baked into the vertex colour at :29, albedo x (1 - `DRAFT-relief-k` 0.6 x (1 - n.y)) with n the vertex normal, and the sun lowered to elevation 30 degrees; (b) contour lines: a cell whose height range crosses a multiple of `DRAFT-contour-step` 100 wu gets its colour darkened by 0.5, giving one dark line per 100 wu of height; (c) both. New: in the land era (`_ground3d != null and not _space_hosted`) `_redraw` (1) hides UnitSquads and TerrainLayer (`visible = false`, set once when the ground host is created; the 3D bodies and the ground mesh are the unit and the terrain), (2) places UnitVisuals through the projector in `set_frame`, not only in `_apply_motion`, so a pan without a step keeps the projected positions (the flat `set_frame` placement is skipped on land: `unit_visuals.gd` gains `set_projector`, the same form as `map_layer.gd:20-26`, and `_to_screen` uses it when valid; the flat branch stays for space), (3) gives StructuresLayer, Deaths, Abilities and Ambient `set_projector(_project_ground)` the way :1322-1326 does, each file gaining the `_projector` form if it lacks it, or hides the node on land when it draws nothing the player needs there (the builder lists which, with the reason, in the report). Tests first (`tests/test_vg_c_land_one_projection.gd`): on the real `tactical_view.tscn` with a ground host and a 12-unit fixture frame at three cameras (default, height 300, and after a pan of 600 wu in +x with no step), every visible node under UnitVisuals and StructuresLayer that carries a unit or structure id lies within 1 px of `Ground3DHost.to_screen(x, y)` of that id; UnitSquads and TerrainLayer are not visible; `sight_discs` key present and empty (one assertion); positive control: on main the squads assertion fails at 263 px. Fixture: the first 12 own units by ascending id from the frame the C plays use (greek placeholder, seed 1, tick 0, terrain from the pipe dump as `s004.py` does); the writer lists the ids and positions in the test header; the default camera height is the rig's start height (`_height := 3000.0`, the evidence page agrees) [P scenes/ground3d/camera_rig_3d.gd:30], which the writer reads from the scene and names in the test. Interface, fixed in full: `qa_state()` gains `marks_px`, a Dictionary keyed by unit id string, each `{"icon": [x, y]}` from the UnitVisuals node's global position plus `"squads": [[x, y], ...]` when UnitSquads is visible, with structures as `marks_px["structure:<id>"] = {"icon": [x, y]}`; and `hidden_on_land`, the Array of node names this piece hides (`["UnitSquads", "TerrainLayer"]` on land, `[]` in space); a hidden node pins `visible == false` and its name in `hidden_on_land`. Deaths, Abilities and Ambient carry no ids, so the test checks that each has a valid `_projector` on land through `has_projector() -> bool`, one method per file (`fx/deaths.gd` already holds `_projector` [P scenes/tactical_c/fx/deaths.gd:31], `fx/abilities.gd` and the Ambient file `fx/ambient.gd` gain it), instead of a position. Camera without a step: no existing `qa_daemon.gd` verb sets a camera pan (the verbs are the `_dispatch` match, and the height-300 shots of the s004 play and run-20261021T221300Z-zoomed were made with wheel input) [P autoload/qa_daemon.gd:220-267], so `qa_daemon.gd` gains verb `camera_set {height?, x?, y?}` that calls the rig (`pan_to` [P scenes/ground3d/camera_rig_3d.gd:123], `camera_height()` [P scenes/ground3d/camera_rig_3d.gd:172]) and redraws without a sim step; height snaps to the rig's wheel ladder; the writer first greps `autoload/qa_daemon.gd` for "camera" and reuses a verb if one has appeared. **LAB-CONTRACT-unit-pos-batch (sim contract; owners per the pieces row; after VG-U8-PREF-WIRE; Architect 2026-10-23, implementation, Westley may veto; a second-Fable audit folds before the test writer). Tool: Workflow (contract piece).** [B R-085-01@773d6a] [P csrc/ouroboros_sim.h:31] [P docs/northstar/spec_factory/signatures.toml:8] [P csrc/tactical_move.c:2127] [P csrc/tactical_move.c:2250] [P csrc/tactical_move.c:2297] [P csrc/tactical_move.c:2428] [P csrc/tactical_move.c:2582] [P csrc/tactical_move.c:3073] Measured basis (a7, 2026-10-23, 26 lab fights; NUMBER-NEEDED: the record, owner the lab overseer a7, who measured, the Architect records: one row in `docs/northstar/research/layer_dynamics/README.md` (or the ledger) with the profiling command (`python3 -m scripts.skilllab <command>` and the profiler used), the 26-fight set, the per-fight call counts (hp, destination, position), the 0.165 share and its split (0.13 destination, 0.035 position), the 0.4827 ceiling and the date, then cited here as `[P docs/northstar/research/layer_dynamics/README.md:<line>]`; candidates if a7's scratch is gone: re-run the profile on main before the test writer starts, or drop the three call counts and keep only the recorded 0.165): the lab's per-unit reads are 338k hp, 240k destination and 100k position calls per fight, 16.5 percent of fight CPU, and no read can be skipped by proof from Python because six C paths clear a destination on any tick (the cites). New, ABI N to N+1 (N read at launch): `typedef struct om_unit_snapshot { double x, y, hp; uint32_t id; int32_t layer; uint8_t alive, has_dest; uint16_t pad; uint32_t pad2; } om_unit_snapshot;` (sizeof 40, 8-aligned, no implicit padding; `pad` and `pad2` written 0; native layout, in-process only, never a pipe frame) and `uint32_t om_unit_snapshot_batch(const om_world *w, om_unit_snapshot *buf, uint32_t cap)`: returns the unit count (`om_unit_count`), fills `min(count, cap)` entries in ascending id order (`om_unit_id_at` order; rows are never freed, so index order is id order, `csrc/unit.c:3090-3094`, `csrc/tactical_move.c:1122`). Per entry: `id` = `om_unit_id_at(i)`; `hp` = `om_unit_get_hp`; `alive` = `om_unit_alive` (hp > 0, `csrc/unit.c:2659-2662`, called through the same cast the single getters use, `tactical_move.c:3154`); `layer` = `om_unit_get_layer`; `x`, `y`, `has_dest` read after one `om_move_sync` through `om_move_get` only (never `om_move_touch`, the `om_move_route_get` pattern, `tactical_move.c:2322-2337`): a row with `in_use` gives `mu->x`, `mu->y`, `mu->has_dest`; no row or `in_use` 0 gives `om_unit_position_x/y` and `has_dest` 0, which is what `om_move_touch` would have produced (`:1085-1093`). A snapshot entry therefore equals the five single calls on the same tick (the test), and the batch never raises `discovery_max` or creates a row. `w` NULL returns 0; `buf` NULL or `cap` 0 counts only (nothing written); `cap` greater than the count leaves entries past the count untouched; `buf` is caller-owned and holds no pointers; the call allocates nothing and has no error code (the count is the whole answer); a dead unit's entry has `alive` 0 and its last values. `signatures.toml`: the `[[symbols]]` row (module tactical_move.c, epics E-13, note: lab-only read batch over the E-13 move rows and the E-11 unit rows) with no `wrapper` (raw `POINTER(om_unit_snapshot)`, so the test can pass NULL and a short buffer) and a `[[structs]]` row `name = "om_unit_snapshot"`, `source_test = "tests/csim/test_LAB_CONTRACT_unit_pos_batch.py (class OMUnitSnapshot)"`, fields in that order; `[meta]` `abi_version`, `symbol_count` and `struct_count` each +1 from their values at launch; the eight ABI pins (`signatures.toml:8`, `tests/csim/conftest.py:64`, `tests/csim/test_E02_S020_abi.py:23`, `tests/csim/test_VG_E_contract.py:74`, `tests/csim/test_VG_E_ke5.py:640`, `tests/specfactory/test_pp_contract.py:29`, `tests/transport/test_pipe_shapes.py:81`, `tests/transport/test_pipe_vg_e.py:195`) and the `docs/architecture.md:215` hello example move N to N+1 once under `TD-VG-E-37`; the header changelog comment (`ouroboros_sim.h:28-30`) gains one line naming the symbol; one `reach_allowlist.toml` line (lab-only symbol, not reachable from the tick by design, stated there); one `docs/architecture.md` subsection `### Lab unit snapshot (LAB-CONTRACT-unit-pos-batch, ABI N+1; lab-only, not a pipe frame)` inserted after `:441` and before `### Schemas and fixture` (:442), giving the struct, the call, the cap and NULL rules and the no-touch read path. Tests first (`tests/csim/test_LAB_CONTRACT_unit_pos_batch.py`): on the lab mirror control `plays.blob_play(16)` (`scripts/skilllab/plays.py`) built as `tests/csim/parity/baseline/baseline_fights.py:128-146` builds it, seed 0, stepped by `Engine.step`, at ticks 0, 100 and after a kill (the kill tick is the first tick at which any `om_unit_alive` reads 0, found by the test, not a literal), every entry equals the single getters; count-only call; cap smaller than count fills cap entries and returns count; ordering by id; `ctypes.sizeof(OMUnitSnapshot) == 40`; the mission hash and the save bytes are unchanged after a batch call on a world with units that have no move row (`om_world_get_state_checksum`, `ouroboros_sim.h:190`, byte-equal before and after the batch call on the 16v16 world at tick 0 before any step and again after a spawn mid-fight); positive controls (`scripts/specfactory/undertest_check.py` mutate_diff on the builder's diff, the W2 pattern, `tests/csim/test_VG_W_w2_routes.py:186-196`): a mutant that skips `has_dest` fails the equality test; a mutant that returns the filled count instead of `om_unit_count` fails the cap test; a mutant that writes index instead of `om_unit_id_at(i)` fails the order test; after the kill, the dead entry's `alive` is 0 and `hp <= 0` while `x`, `y` equal the single getters (a mutant that zeroes a dead row's position fails); the checksum control: the same two checksums differ when the single getters are called instead (the touch path, `tactical_move.c:1115`). The consumer piece (lab side, `scripts/skilllab/fight.py` and `firing.py`, after this lands) replaces the per-unit loops with one snapshot per tick and keeps FightResult byte-equal against `ref_fight_rev2.py` and `ref_firing_rev2.py`; its mark is a new draft from the measured ceiling (0.4827 both levers), named at its launch.

**VG-E-prodslot (lane E1, after VG-CONTRACT-E7 lands; owner `csrc/tactical_support.c` production spawn lines, `csrc/mission.c` slot helper, `csrc/om_internal.h` one prototype plus its signatures row and EXPECTED_MODULE line, `tests/csim/test_VG_E_prodslot.py`).** `[P csrc/tactical_support.c:546-547: a produced unit lands at a random angle and a distance uniform in [0, OM_REINFORCEMENT_CAPTURE_RADIUS / 2 = 150 wu) from the structure, no occupancy check]` `[P csrc/unit.c:2980: om_unit_spawn checks terrain only]` `[P csrc/mission.c:2350, 2470: mission start uses a 30 wu slot lattice with used-slot checks; production never calls it]` `FINDING-production-stack`. Production places each new unit at the first free slot, in a fixed scan order, of a lattice centred on the structure at the spacing mission start uses (2 x OM_UNIT_COLLISION_RADIUS, `mission.c:2470`): slots P + (i s, j s), the origin excluded, rings r = max(|i|, |j|) scanned outward and row-major within a ring (not nearest-first past ring 3, by design), a slot counting when its distance from the structure is at most 150 wu (half the capture radius, inclusive) and free when no live unit stands strictly within one spacing of it (squared distances, no epsilon) and the terrain would accept a spawn there; helper `int om_mission_production_slot(const om_world *w, uint32_t structure, double *out_x, double *out_y)` in `mission.c`, declared in `om_internal.h`, bound for the isolation test through `tests/csim/conftest.py`; no free slot within 150 wu delays production one tick (the structure keeps its timer). Deterministic: scan order fixed, no RNG draw consumed (the two RNG draws at :546-547 are removed, so the RNG stream changes: goldens with production move once under `TD-VG-E-29`, rule-changed by Westley 2026-10-12, old and new named; goldens without production must not move, the Goldens step verifies). The Docs step rewrites R-126-02, E-15 line 184, ALG-75's spawn clause and the closed register row Q-126-02 to the lattice rule (texts in the args gap g3), and the eight tests that cite R-126-02 or last_spawn_distance get the new rule in their docstrings, assertions untouched (all bound the spawn at 150 wu inclusive). A golden both known-red under TD-VG-E-26 and moved here keeps its known-red line with the reason extended to both rows until startforce lands. Tests first: ten productions from one structure yield ten units pairwise at least 30 wu apart (red today: paste the first pair under 30); a structure with every slot within 150 wu occupied delays one tick and places when a slot frees; a save round trip mid-sequence gives the same positions; control: mission-start spacing unchanged.

The display waiver FU-B22-pregate-display extends to `tests/test_vg_b_b24.gd` (an xvfb-run line in place of PRE_GATE, as B23). `[W 2026-10-12: civilizations congregate regionally, random regions are against the rules]` `[P scenes/galaxy/galaxy_gen.gd _place_one: regions placed without reading the graph]` `[P measured 2026-10-12 under Forward+, seed 1: B23 sim edges median 54 wu, mean 53.6, 55 of 88 over 50 wu in a ball of radius 50; prototype edges median 13.07, min 11.0, max 18.0 over 398 edges]` Placement follows the sim graph: regions are placed in breadth-first order from the region of highest degree (ties by lower id); each region goes at the centroid of its already-placed neighbours plus a hashed direction at a distance drawn by hash from [11, 18] wu times `CIV_SCALE`-relative units as the prototype measures them, rejected and re-hashed (up to 16 tries) when nearer than 11 wu to any placed region or outside the ball; then 20 relaxation passes (edges spring toward 13 wu, pairs under 11 wu repel), all from the seed, no engine RNG. A disconnected component starts at the hashed free position farthest from placed regions. Significance is continuous: `sig = 0.3 + 0.5 * rank / (n - 1)` by region capacity (ties by id) `[DRAFT-b24-sig]`, replacing `SIGNIFICANCE_BY_TIER`. Parity test (frame, not constants): at seed 1 the sim-edge median lies in [11, 18]; no edge over 30 wu; the Overview frame's filament pixel coverage (pixels whose max(R, G, B) is above 200, discs masked at their drawn radius, Forward+, fixture `origin/push/galaxy-field` 99f35887 seed 1, Overview at d25 = camera distance 25, target origin, default pitch) is at most 1.5 times the prototype's `[DRAFT-b24-filament-cover]`; discs on screen at d25 (a disc counts when its projected centre is inside the viewport) at least 18 (B23's measured count) and the measured number reported `[DRAFT-b24-discs]` (old value 30, struck `[A 2026-10-12, drafter gap ruling]`; the prototype shows about 45 with 217 systems; the density question is Westley's, `Q-B24-01`: (a) unowned decoration systems to the prototype's count; (b) 80 is the look; (c) a sim change to more regions; open until he has looked at the prototype and the game).
